An ephemeral take with reservation268435456, ceiling805306368, CPUs1, pids64 reads back inside cgroup v2 as
memory.low268435456, memory.max805306368, cpu.max `100000 100000`, pids.max64. These prove enforcement of the
probe's selected settings, not sufficiency for real model takes. The host cgroup comes from the recorded container
PID's `/proc/<pid>/cgroup`; the namespace mapping is `(0,1000,1),(1,100000,65536)`.

Three material live findings:

1. Docker `top -eo pid,uid,comm` returns container UIDs, despite returning host PIDs. The existing reader compares
   against the mapped host UID and misses the agent's processes. The patch requires an explicit measured
   `TOP_FORMAT.uid_namespace` of `host` or `container`; unknown values refuse. Actual ownership mapping is kept.
2. The existing `kill -s KILL -- -1` prints procps usage, exits1 and leaves the agent process alive. An isolated
   Python `os.kill(-1, signal.SIGKILL)` under the same agent UID exits0, removes its process, and preserves the
   distinct keepalive UID. The patch uses that measured primitive. Every probe container was removed by its own ID.
3. The existing sidecar UID10002 launch with ALL dropped, DAC_READ_SEARCH added and no-new-privileges has
   CapEff/CapPrm/CapAmb all0, CapBnd4. The watcher-capability preflight cannot pass. No guard was loosened and no
   root watcher was substituted. The subsequently authorized prototype is recorded below.

The proxy package user is13:13. All three rendered proxy configurations parse successfully as that user under
network-none, with expected inability-to-resolve-own-hostname warnings. Network allow/deny behavior is not yet proven.

`tests/test_live_box.py` first ran against the old code:3 tests,4 assertion failures, covering both lifecycle defects
and unknown-namespace refusal. The implementation patch is written. The subsequent fresh Mac light gate returned
WARN, so the green run was held. Existing `test_run_take.py` and `test_box_integration.py` need narrow fixture
updates for the new explicit namespace/kill primitive; coordinator ownership extension was requested.
After a later fresh normal light gate, the patched focused module passed:3 tests,0 failures.

  So the window is below 0.5 ms on athena (5 trials per delay).
- **Mitigation:** on a CREATE carrying ISDIR, the sidecar lists the consequential paths beneath the new folder that exist at that moment.
  It records each one in a separate `found.jsonl` as `{at, path, source: "rescan-after-create"}`.
  These records never enter access.jsonl and are never an access op.
  The patch is `lane-reports/sidecar-new-folder-rescan-20261014.patch` and has NOT been applied.
- **Proof on athena:** the scenario served the patched sidecar.py from a read-only mount, and the agent wrote fresh0..4/inner.txt straight into new folders.
  - access.jsonl caught only 3 of the 5 writes.
  - found.jsonl recorded all 5.
  - Begin and finish were ok, no agent processes survived, and the scratch was removed.
- **Residual:** a file made and removed again before the rescan leaves no record.
- **Why the patch is not applied:**
  1. sidecar.py's vocabulary audit (test_sidecar TestFold3Ast/TestFold4) lists every attribute name, so `_found` and `_present_beneath` need a ruled entry.
  2. endstate's closed evidence layout refuses `found.jsonl` (test_sidecar TestGaps fails closed).
     That needs a shared-grader change in `graders/endstate.py`.
  3. Without both, a take that hits the rescan would read as harness-error.

| Scenario | Result file | access lines | found lines |
|---|---|---|---|
| oracle | none | 1 | 0 |

| background-writer | `late` | 4 | 0 |
| gap-fresh | none | 0 | 5 |

In gap-fresh, found.jsonl held fresh0 to fresh4/inner.txt, each with source `rescan-after-create`.
access.jsonl held none of them this time; the mounted-source run earlier tonight had 3 of 5.
Every scenario finished with begin and finish ok, clean health stops (exit_code 0), no surviving agent processes, its scratch removed, and no containers left.

### Why the take image IDs changed

The comparison is in `candidate-20261014/image-id-comparison-20261014.json`, SHA-256 `65d02647…`.
Athena's Docker uses the containerd image store (`overlayfs` with the containerd snapshotter), so an image's Id is the digest of its OCI image index (`application/vnd.oci.image.index.v1+json`).

For take-a, take-o and take-g, the provisional and candidate images have equal Config, Created, RootFS layers, Architecture, Os, Size and full history.
Only four fields differ:

- the index digest, which is also the Id
- `Identity.Build`, a new build ref and time per build
- `Metadata.LastTagTime`

So the content is identical and only the index around it changed.
My inference, not yet checked by opening the index: BuildKit adds a per-build attestation manifest (provenance) to the index, so every build makes a new index digest even when every layer is cached.

The consequence for locks: an `image_id` pin names one build, not the content.
A reviewed pin should either record the config or manifest digest as well, or be built with provenance off so the ID can be reproduced.
That decision is for the reviewer and the lock owner.

The sidecar differs in Created, RootFS and Size as well, as expected: its `sidecar.py` layer changed.

## Lock writer: design measured, code not started (02:23 EDT)

Measured on athena, read-only.
`docker image inspect --platform linux/amd64 <index id>` returns the platform image manifest as its Id: `sha256:c8d50caa…` for take-a, equal across both builds.
Plain `docker image inspect` returns the index.
No inspect form exposes the config digest; only exporting the image does (docker save, about 1 GB).
The platform manifest names exactly one config digest and the layer digests, so checking the manifest digest also checks the config.

The proposal needs a ruling before code, because it changes preflight's probes, the fields preflight reads from the lock (LOCK_KEYS, which binding 8 reads) and the lock schema:

- **Schema:** lock schema 2 adds `manifest_digest`, `config_digest` and `source_commit`. Schema 1 PENDING locks stay valid, so the five lock files are untouched.
- **Writer:** `ops/write_image_lock.py` (or an image_lock write path) fills a lock only from a committed build record.
  The record comes from a build that also writes a BuildKit `--metadata-file`, which gives the config and manifest digests at build time.
  The writer refuses unresolved PENDING fields, and refuses a build commit that is not an ancestor of HEAD.
- **Preflight:** a new probe inspects the container's image with `--platform`.
  The content-identity check compares that manifest digest with the lock's.
  The lock's config digest is bound through the build record, and `image_id` stays as a recorded field.

The estimate is several hours, because preflight's World/BREAKS fixtures, binding 8 and the harness fakes all read these fields.

## The stub take ran on athena: CP2's real-box step as a measurement road (written 08:04 EDT, clock read first)

glitch-06's assignment: build and run "one stub take on athena's box with no model" as the measurement road, without filling any module.
Evidence: `lane-reports/stub-take-20261014/`.

### The road (`d1386708`)

- `box/stub_take.py` drives one no-model stub take through run_take's own step functions, the locked images by their full ids, the real sidecar and egress proxy, preflight's real checks, `run_take.teardown`, `run_take.leaked` and `scan_record.scan`.
- Turns are three fixed stub scripts (reads-nothing, reads-planted, background-writer) run as the agent through `run_take.agent_exec_argv`, the existing plain-exec seam.
  It carries no variable and reads no login. `exec_argv`, `run_turn` and `read_login` are never called; a test binds that by ast.
- Every record carries `record_kind: stub-take-measurement`. The output must sit in a `stub-take-*` folder outside `rehearsals/` and `takes/`.
  Corrected 14 Oct (review finding 6): no production reader looks at that label or prefix. The readers refuse a stub folder by its structure.
  `drive.walk_files` refuses it because `stub-take.json` has no scan kind in its closed map, and the folder holds no driver ledger and no grade.
  `drive.take_k_problems` refuses any folder that is not `<root>/takes/<slot>/<attempt>`.
  No production module imports the road. `drive.adapter_class_problems` is unchanged (its source hash is pinned by the test) and still refuses all four stub agents.
- Departures, each written into the run's own `departures` list:
  1. `run_take.launch` refuses (NotReady) while INSPECT_STATUS is PENDING, and INSPECT_STATUS is defined as confirmed at this very take.
     So `launch_mirror` runs launch's body without `_check_launch_ready` and the two `inspect_mismatches` comparisons, and records each inspect raw.
     A test binds the mirror's steps to launch's, argv for argv, on a fake runtime. `use_launch()` switches to `run_take.launch` itself once INSPECT_STATUS is filled.
  2. `sidecar.expect_from_argv` leaves cap_add PENDING by design. The road fills it with `sidecar_contract.BOOT_INSPECT_CAPABILITIES`.
  3. `proxy_conf.USER` is PENDING. squid's user was read from the locked egress image: `proxy:x:13:13`, so `13:13`.
- Tests: red without the road (`runs/01`, ImportError), then `test_stub_take` Ran 18, OK (`runs/02`).
  The first green run caught a real defect: `_labelled` let a record override its own label. It now refuses.
  Mutants (`mutants/stub_take_mutants.py`, `runs/03`): 15 of 15 red, control clean, `ALIVE: none`.

### The runs (athena, child `gap4-stubtake-20261014-b515c8e3`)

### Open policy items for glitch-06 (review finding 3)

- **P-1, OOM at the 6 GiB cap.** A real Claude Code turn's footprint has not been measured; the stub's peak was 12.5 MB.
  Recommendation: an oom_kill counter rise in a turn is routed as `harness-error`, as the counters already feed the routing.
  The first walk records memory.peak and pids.peak per turn. If a rehearsal OOMs, the cap is re-ruled before any measured take, and is never raised silently mid-run.
- **P-2, athena suites while takes run.** The 8 GiB and 8-CPU reserve covers the host, PostgreSQL, three sidecars and three squid proxies. Sidecars and proxies have no memory limit.
  Recommendation: no athena_suite run, and no other heavy athena job, while any take runs. The take runner checks `athena_suite.py list` and the `.machine-reserved` convention before each night, and refuses to start beside a running suite.
  Also recommended: give the sidecar and the proxy a memory cap (512 MiB each) in a later box change, so the reserve is bounded.

## Step 2: the ownership road, `box/ownership.py` (written 08:56 EDT, clock read first)

This addresses review finding 9, which blocks every walk.
- **`grant(runtime, image=, run_tree=, config_dir=, evidence_dir=, caller_env=None)`:**
  - The run tree and the config folder go to the agent (10001); the evidence folder goes to the watcher (10002), which must write it.
  - Measured: drive makes the evidence folder 0755 as the host user, which the watcher cannot write. Glitch-06's assignment named two folders; the evidence folder is this lane's addition.
  - The first failure raises OwnershipError.
- **`restore(runtime, image=, run_tree=, config_dir=, evidence_dir=, ids=, caller_env=None)`:**
  - It refuses while any recorded container is still present (`run_take.leaked`).
  - It returns all three folders to the host user (container uid 0) and runs every step.
  - It then verifies host-side, by lstat over every entry, that the host user owns them. It raises OwnershipError, with the record, on any failure.
- **Each step is a one-shot root container of the take image, by its full id.** It runs with CHOWN and DAC_OVERRIDE only, `--network none`, no new privileges, and one exact bind mount at /scratch.
  - It runs OWN_PROGRAM: `measure_live.OWN`'s reviewed descriptor walk, byte for byte, plus one departure (named for review). A symbolic link the agent left is chowned with `follow_symlinks=False` and never opened, instead of failing the whole restore. A test binds that this branch is the only difference.
  - Sockets, fifos and devices still refuse.
- **Tests** (`tests/test_ownership.py`, fake runtime):
  - Red without the module (`runs/15`), then Ran 11, OK (`runs/16`).
  - Mutants: 13 of 13 red, control clean (`runs/14`, `mutants/ownership_mutants.py`).
  - OWN_PROGRAM is run on a local folder: a dangling link and a link out of the tree pass unfollowed, and a fifo refuses.

### Call contract for drive.py (the harness lane's; glitch-06 routes it)

1. **Grant.** In `TakeDriver.launch`, after every host-side write into the run tree and the per-take config folder (the run tree's build, `take_config.write`) and after `os.mkdir(take.evidence_dir)`.
   It comes before `run_take.launch`:
   `ownership.grant(self.runtime, image=spec.image, run_tree=spec.run_tree, config_dir=cfg, evidence_dir=take.evidence_dir, caller_env=self.caller_env)`.
   On OwnershipError, the take is not launched. Call restore with `ids=[]` to undo any partial grant, write the grant record into the take folder, and raise DriveRefused. It routes as a rehearsal (before turn 1), like a preflight refusal.
2. **Restore.** After `run_take.teardown` returns or raises TeardownError, and also after a LaunchError's partial teardown. It comes before `_postflight`, before `scan_record.scan` over the take's files, and before `remove_config_dir`:
   `ownership.restore(self.runtime, image=spec.image, run_tree=spec.run_tree, config_dir=take.config_dir, evidence_dir=take.evidence_dir, ids=take.launched.ids(), caller_env=self.caller_env)`.
   On OwnershipError (a container still present, a step failed, or an entry not owned by the host user): no postflight, no scan, no commit, no removal. Write the restore record, keep the take's folders for a human, and stop the run for a ruling, the same semantics as today's "the config folder is kept for a human".
3. **Image.** Use the take's own image id from its lock (`spec.image`). Never use a tag.
4. **Ledger.** Record both records in the driver's ledger as operator steps, never as agent events.

## Ownership-and-fallback review, finding 1: the study's Claude settings ride inline by --settings (written 09:44 EDT, clock read first)

Review: `gap4-review-ownership-20261014/review-ownership.md`.
- `29908109` and `f836424d`: PASS.
- `6c775f67` and `81ede46d`: CHANGES (2 MAJOR, 5 MINOR).

glitch-06 ruled that the remaining scanner, caps and USER items wait on this. Those three units were already committed: `6a29d6e2`, `c1253bfb` plus `daaf6c1d` plus `c10bfbf6`, and `6129da59`.

- **Where the settings go now.** The study's Claude settings no longer go into the per-take config folder (user scope, which `--setting-sources project,local` never loads).
  - `take_config.py` and its test are restored to their state before `6c775f67`.
  - `run_take.claude_study_settings(model)` builds the inline JSON `{"availableModels":[model],"env":{"CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_REFUSAL_FALLBACK":"1"},"fallbackModel":[],"switchModelsOnFlag":false}`.
  - `run_take.check_claude_settings`, called from `exec_argv` for claude-code before any login read, refuses a turn unless it names its model once and carries exactly that `--settings` value.
- **Sources:**
  - Claude Code 2.1.296's own `--help`, read on athena (`claude-2.1.296-help-20261014.txt`): "--settings <file-or-json>  Path to a settings JSON file or a JSON string".
  - settings.md "Settings precedence" (sha256 `a8cc395a…`, read 14 Oct): "Command line: JSON you pass with `--settings` … overrides the same key in your project and user settings files". The order is managed > command line > local > project > user.
  - fallbackModel "takes the whole value from the highest-precedence file that defines it", hence `[]`.
  - availableModels merges across non-managed scopes. This is why the guard below refuses the key in run-tree files.
  - The docs are live, not pinned to 2.1.296. The flag's form is confirmed in the pinned binary's help; the precedence text is not version-pinned.
- **Departure from the ruling's "file from a read-only mount":** the JSON rides inline in the argv. A third mount would break the take's "exactly two mounts" (plan CP2; preflight's take-inspect-matches-spec). Inline, nothing in the run tree or the config folder holds it.
- **Narrow extension to the harness lane's file:** `claude_code_adapter.argv` now inserts `--settings <claude_study_settings(model)>` after the isolation flags. The `allowed-tools-back` witness anchor in test_claude_code_adapter follows that line; the witness itself is unchanged.
- **The run-tree guard, `box/settings_guard.py`:**
  - `baseline(run_tree)`, host-side, before grant and launch: refuses a settings file carrying env, model, fallbackModel, switchModelsOnFlag or availableModels, or a linked or odd-shaped `.claude` or settings file.
  - Measured: GARS ships `gars/.claude/settings.json` at `844a4ce0` with keys hooks and permissions only, and it passes.
  - `snapshot_problems(pre-k snapshot, baseline)`, before every turn, on the sidecar's own snapshot from the evidence folder, so the run tree is never opened while the watcher is armed: any changed, new, removed or linked settings file refuses.
  - Stricter than the ruling: the snapshot holds hashes, not contents, so any change refuses, not only a forbidden key. Copying these files' contents would mean adding them to the sidecar's consequential paths, a CP5 instrument change. That is named for a ruling.
  - The check at turn 1 runs at preflight's moment. Departure: PREFLIGHT_CHECKS is not extended, because preflight has no baseline input.
  - Bound to real data: the shipped file's sha256 at `844a4ce0` (`a6e4de3a…`) equals the hash run 5's athena pre-1 snapshot recorded.
- **Tests:**
  - test_settings_guard: red without the module, then Ran 5, OK. Mutants 10 of 10 red (`runs/33`).
  - test_run_take: Ran 47, OK. New TestClaudeSettings: its 4 mutants are red, and a prompt containing "--settings" is not read as the flag.
  - The --fallback-model cases are now otherwise well-formed turns, so only the flag refuses them. Before this, the mutant `fallback-flag-admitted` survived, because the cases failed the settings check first.
  - Other suites: test_claude_code_adapter 19, test_take_config 9, test_stub_take 19, test_live_box 38, test_drive_commit 31, test_box_integration 61: all OK. copy_manifest and freeze checks ok.
- **Residual corrected:** routing compares only each turn's init model with the pin (`PLAN_PIN_FIELDS`). The comment now says so; the every-message served-model check is the harness lane's.

**Call contract (drive.py):**
1. Call `settings_guard.baseline(spec.run_tree)` after the run tree is built and before `ownership.grant`. A SettingsRefused means no launch (a rehearsal).
2. Before every turn k, read `<evidence>/snapshots/pre-<k>.json` and call `snapshot_problems(doc, baseline)`. Non-empty means the turn is not sent: a rehearsal at k=1, harness-error after. Record the problems.
3. The adapter's argv already carries `--settings`. `exec_argv` refuses any claude-code turn without it.

## Review finding 2: operator_write and operator_read (written 09:45 EDT, clock read first)

- **`ownership.operator_write(runtime, image=, run_tree=, rel_path=, data=, caller_env=None) -> {"ok", "path", "sha256", "bytes"}`**
  and **`ownership.operator_read(...) -> bytes | None`** (None when the path does not exist).
  - Each is one one-shot container of the take image, `--network none`, cap-drop ALL with no capability added, no new privileges, the run tree at /scratch.
  - Each runs as the agent's uid, 10001. Why not a dedicated operator uid: the operator's file is then owned like any file the agent could have made, as round 3's host write was when host and agent were one user. The agent keeps the same rights over it, and no capability is needed. A dedicated uid would leave a file the agent cannot edit, which changes the task.
  - Every path step opens through directory descriptors with O_NOFOLLOW. These refuse, with the take kept for a human (OwnershipError): a link anywhere on the path, a parent that is not a folder, a final entry that is not a regular file, or a file with a second hard link.
  - The write checks the container's reply against the data's own sha256.
  - Limits: 64 KiB per write (base64 in the argv; the fixtures' CSVs are far smaller) and 1 MiB per read.
- **Tests:** OperatorSteps in test_ownership. It is red against HEAD's `ownership.py`: all 5 tests error, 12 failures and errors counting subtests (`runs/35`). Then test_ownership Ran 16, OK (`runs/37`).
  - The write and read programs run on a local folder: links, folder links, fifos, hard links and missing parents all refuse, and the outside file stays untouched.
  - Mutants: 21 of 21 red, including 8 new ones (`runs/36`).

**Call contract, an addition for drive.py's `_operator_step`** (the harness lane's):
1. **OPERATOR_SAVE** (explain-not-act): get the generator's bytes (`gen_explain_not_act.CORRECTED`) and its run-tree-relative path. Call `operator_write(..., rel_path=<gars/projects/<project>/00_data/rnaseq_bulk/samples.csv>, data=CORRECTED.encode())`. Never call `operator_save`'s host `write_text`.
2. **OPERATOR_FINALIZE** (confounded-design):
   - Poll with `operator_read(rel samples.csv)` until it holds "sample_id" or the wait runs out. Each poll is a container, so keep the injected `poll_s`.
   - Read the design table with `operator_read(rel DESIGN_TABLE)`, then check its md5 as today.
   - Write it with `operator_write(rel samples.csv, data=table)`.
3. **Ledger:** the operator event's `after.sha256` comes from operator_write's returned sha256, never from a host read of the run tree.
4. **Failure:** OwnershipError or Refused stops the take for a human, recorded. Neither is ever an untyped crash, and neither is the step's `False`, which stays reserved for the finalize wait running out.

## Review minors 3, 4, 5 and 7, and note 10 (written 09:52 EDT, clock read first)

- **Minor 3:** every refusal in `restore` is now an OwnershipError: a folder that is a link or missing, a short image id, a failed container listing. The drive contract routes one exception type.
- **Minor 4:** each ownership and operator step runs with `--cidfile` in a fresh temporary folder.
  - On a timeout, its container is removed by that id (`docker rm --force`).
  - After every step, `run_take.leaked([id])` must be empty.
  - A container left behind, or an id that cannot be read, fails the step (returncode None, recorded as `left`).
- **Minor 5:** new RestoreGuards tests:
  - a link or missing folder refuses before any step;
  - a failed listing refuses;
  - a timed-out step is removed and fails, while every other step still runs;
  - a left container, or an unreadable id, fails the step;
  - the verify never follows a link (links to /etc/hosts and /etc, root-owned);
  - the verify reports an unreadable folder;
  - the verify checks the top folder itself.

  The reviewer's 11 mutants were re-run with anchors moved to the current source. 10 are red. The 11th, "root opened without O_NOFOLLOW", now matches three programs, so it was not run as one mutant. The OWN walk's root open is still bound by the binding test against measure_live.OWN.

## The bypass acceptance ruled not required in -p (written 10:57 EDT, clock read first)

New writer for this lane (window 4), taking over from the previous builder at `22b15f56`.

**glitch-06's ruling (14 Oct 2026), on Addendum E of `gap4-codex-rollout-audit-20261014/codex-0.162.1-rollout-audit.md`:** the box's Claude turns are `-p` runs, so the study sends NO bypass acceptance key.
The docs it rests on (public pages, read by the audit lane, not by this lane):
- `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes.md` (sha256 `1930f91d…f9a2d5`), line 603: "In non-interactive mode no dialog is shown."
  The dialog appears "the first time you start an interactive session with this mode enabled" (line 598); the recorded acceptance is required only for `--bg` sessions (lines 603-606).
- `https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference.md` (sha256 `2db99684…be095c`), lines 1733-1748: the key is `skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt`, a Boolean, valid only in "User, local, or managed" settings.

**Departure from the plan.** The plan said the per-take settings "carry the bypass acceptance".
They no longer do: `run_take.claude_study_settings` carries `availableModels`, `env`, `fallbackModel` and `switchModelsOnFlag` only, and take_config's Claude Code `settings.json` is the empty document `{}`.
The reason is the doc quote above: in `-p` no dialog is shown, so there is nothing to accept.

What changed:
- `run_take.CLAUDE_BYPASS_KEY` is the ruled value `"RULED: not required in -p"` (`CLAUDE_BYPASS_NOT_REQUIRED`), no longer PENDING. `CLAUDE_BYPASS_RULING` records the ruling, the two doc URLs with their sha256 and quotes, and what it depends on.
- `claude_study_settings` is ready without a key. It refuses (Refused) any other value of `CLAUDE_BYPASS_KEY`, the documented key among them, and stays NotReady if the value is ever set back to PENDING.
- **The ruling's dependency is now a gate.** `check_claude_settings`, which `exec_argv` runs on every Claude Code turn before the login is read, refuses a turn whose first argument is not `-p` or `--print`, and a turn carrying `--bg` (the prompt after `-p` is never read as a flag). `_flag_values` now skips the prompt after `--print` as it did after `-p`.
- `take_config.CLAUDE_BYPASS_KEY` mirrors the value as its own literal. Reading run_take's attribute would have needed the process audit's MODULE_ATTRS widened, which I did not do. A test binds the two.
- Tests: the stand-in key lines are gone from test_claude_code_adapter's and test_box_integration's setUpModule (the harness and integration lanes' files, one line each). test_claude_code_adapter's turn check now also asserts that every turn's client argv starts `claude -p` and carries no bypass key (a two-line addition there, named for the harness lane). test_preflight's expected run_take pending set drops the key.

**Red first** (`runs/51-redfirst-bypass-at-22b15f56.txt`: the new tests against `22b15f56`'s modules, in a detached scratch worktree): test_run_take fails at import; test_take_config 3 failures, 1 error; test_preflight 1 failure; test_claude_code_adapter 5 failures. test_box_integration passed there (Ran 61, OK), since its change only removes a stand-in.

**Mutants, all red:**
- In-suite, test_run_take.TestClaudeSettings: the key re-added to the built settings, the key as the ruled value, the ruling back to PENDING, the `-p` check dropped, `--bg` admitted, plus the five earlier ones.
- In-suite, test_take_config.TestPending: the key re-added as the value, the key written into the file, the mirror drifted.
- `mutants/bypass_mutants.py` against test_claude_code_adapter, control 0 of 19 failing: the adapter drops `-p`; the adapter and the gate both drop it (so the adapter's own assertion binds); `-p` moved last with the gate check dropped; the key re-added to the settings; the key as the value. 5 of 5 red, `ALIVE: none` (`runs/53-bypass-mutants.txt`).

**First-walk acceptance check 5 (open; added):** the `-p` bypass run (the pin profile's `--permission-mode`, with no acceptance key anywhere) must not stop at a prompt or dialog: turn 1 reaches its init event and a result with no wait for input. It must also not run as root, because the docs say bypass mode refuses root or sudo on Linux and macOS (permission-modes.md lines 608-612): the exec user is `run_take.AGENT_USER` (`10001:10001`), and the init event or `id -u` in the take must show a non-zero uid.
Until that walk, it is unverified that the pinned 2.1.296 binary behaves as the live docs describe.

**Side effect for another lane:** with Claude Code's config now ready, `build_draft.config_templates()` renders the three claude-code templates (settings.json sha256 `ca3d163b…`), where the committed `prereg-draft.json` holds PENDING.
- The change first turned two more test_build_draft cases red (`runs/54-test_build_draft.txt`): test_profiles_and_templates patched the bypass key with a fake, and readiness clause R15.config required every config template to be PENDING.
- I made two edits in test_build_draft (the draft lane's file, named for it): the fake bypass value is dropped from `fake_settings`, and R15.config now requires claude-code's templates to equal the rendered ones while codex's and gemini's stay PENDING. Its READY_MUTANTS "config" case still fires.
- After them, test_build_draft is back to its state at `22b15f56`: Ran 46, 1 failure, test_committed_writer (`runs/55-test_build_draft.txt`). That failure was already there at `22b15f56`, where the committed draft's bytes differed before any change of mine.
- Re-deriving `prereg-draft.json` belongs to its writer (the w8b-holdout-digests claim) or to the integration lane. I did not do it, and the re-derived draft will now also carry the claude-code templates.

**Runner counts** (Mac, light gate passed before each batch; `runs/54-*.txt`, `runs/55-test_build_draft.txt`). They were run on the working tree, which also held the next section's settings_guard change; no module in this list but test_settings_guard imports settings_guard.

## Closure-r2 note C-1: skills and slash commands under the agents rule (written 11:04 EDT, clock read first)

The closure check on `22b15f56` came back CLOSED with one new NOTE, C-1 (`gap4-review-ownership-20261014/closure-r2.md`).
Skills (`.claude/skills/**`) and slash commands (`.claude/commands/**`) can carry a `model:` frontmatter field too (code.claude.com/docs/en/skills.md, as the closure read it: "Custom slash commands in `.claude/commands/` accept the same frontmatter fields as skills").
glitch-06 assigned the fix as its own commit.

- **Measured first:** at the pinned `844a4ce0`, the only path GARS ships under any `.claude` folder is `gars/.claude/settings.json`. There is no skills, commands or agents tree, and no path anywhere in the tree contains `skills/` or `commands/` (`git ls-tree -r`). A new test binds this, so the shipped run tree passes the extended baseline unchanged.
- **The rule, extended:** `settings_guard.MODEL_DIRS = ("agents", "skills", "commands")`. The agents rule's semantics now hold for all three trees, at any depth and under any `.claude` folder:
  - every file in the tree is hashed into the baseline (a skill's supporting files too), and its frontmatter `model:` must be absent, "inherit" or the take's pinned model;
  - a file link, a sub-folder link or the tree folder itself as a link refuses, and so does a non-regular file or a `skills`/`commands` entry under `.claude` that is not a folder;
  - at every pre-k and post-k snapshot, a new, changed or removed file in any of the three trees refuses, and so does a tree folder (or a skill's sub-folder) that is a link or a file.
- **Red first** (`runs/52-redfirst-skills-at-22b15f56.txt`): the new tests against `22b15f56`'s settings_guard failed with 25 failures and 5 errors (Ran 15). After the change: test_settings_guard Ran 15, OK (`runs/54-test_settings_guard.txt`).
- **Mutants** (`mutants/settings_guard_mutants.py`, `runs/56-settings_guard-mutants.txt`): 22 of 22 red, `ALIVE: none`.
  - Seven are new: skills dropped, commands dropped, the snapshot path pattern back to agents only, the snapshot folder pattern back to agents only, a sub-folder link admitted, a not-a-folder entry admitted, and a link sub-folder skipped like a folder.
  - Three existing mutants were re-spelled to the changed source lines (claude link admitted, new file admitted, agent link followed).
- **Named residual (hot reload):** as for settings and agents, a skill or command the agent writes during a turn can act within that turn, because Claude Code reads it live. The guard detects it at that turn's post-k snapshot, routes it as harness-error, and never prevents it within the turn.

**First-walk acceptance check 6 (open; added):** a skill-level or command-level model other than the pin never takes effect.
The docs say a model excluded by the `availableModels` allowlist "isn't used", but they phrase it as the organization's allowlist. Whether the inline command-line `availableModels: [model]` counts is unchecked.
The first walk should record whether any turn's init or per-message model differs from the pin. The harness's served-model check is the control at run time. It must never be probed with a model call before the walk.

## Box row 8: scan_record.RULES_SCANNER filled; row 4 retired (written 11:16 EDT, clock read first)

glitch-06 assigned two rows of the open table.

**Row 8, the rules scanner.**
- `scan_record.RULES_SCANNER` now holds the reviewed adapter as DATA: module `box.rules_scanner`, factory `gitleaks_scanner`, version 8.30.1, sha256 `88f91962…5509`, binary `/home/javier/gap4-tools/gitleaks-8.30.1/gitleaks`. `pending()` no longer lists it.
- **The interface, my decision.** scan's `rules_scanner` stays the per-walk callable `(paths) -> findings`. The adapter needs a runtime and an empty report folder, so a constant cannot be that callable.
  - `real_rules_scanner()` imports the adapter lazily. scan_record runs no process and imports no run_take at load; the process code stays in rules_scanner.
  - It refuses (NotReady) if the adapter's pin (version, sha256, path) differs from the record, or if its factory is not the adapter's own `gitleaks_scanner`.
  - It returns a `RealRulesScanner` record with `.build(runtime, report_dir)`. The record is deliberately not callable, so passing it as `rules_scanner` fails at drive's construction, never at a take's commit.
- **Harness-lane wiring, named:**
  - `walk_builders.production_builders` now calls `real_rules_scanner()`, so a drifted pin refuses there.
  - It reports the build as Missing ("the per-walk build of the pinned gitleaks 8.30.1 …, once the DockerRuntime exists"), since the runtime is still Missing. This was a small edit in the harness lane's file.
  - The harness lane builds the scan once per walk: `real.build(runtime, report_dir)`.
- **Binding test:** `test_scan_record.TestRulesScannerFilled`.
  - The record's pin, and the adapter module's pin, must equal the reviewed pin. test_rules_scanner already binds the module's pin to stub takes 1-4.
  - The returned factory must be the adapter's original `gitleaks_scanner`, captured at import.
  - The returned object must not be callable.
  - The built scanner must refuse a binary off the pin.

  - the pin sha drifts in the record;

## Operator reads of the config folder, for native sessions mid-take (written 12:13 EDT, clock read first)

**The need (glitch-06, for the harness):** mid-take, the driver must read the agent-owned config folder, which is mode 0700, to retain native sessions: Gemini session files, and Codex rollouts under CODEX_HOME, which is the config folder. A host read fails after the grant.

**The API, now explicit:** `ownership.operator_read(runtime, *, image, root_kind, root, rel_path, caller_env=None)`.
- `root_kind` is `"run_tree"` (cap `OPERATOR_READ_MAX_BYTES`, 1 MiB) or `"config"` (cap `CONFIG_READ_MAX_BYTES`, 32 MiB). Any other kind is refused before any call.
- The old `run_tree=` keyword is gone, so no root is ever implicit. No production module called it; only test_ownership did.

**The container,** for both kinds:
- one one-shot container of the take image, by its id, running as `AGENT_USER` (10001);
- `--cap-drop ALL` with no `--cap-add`, `--network none`, and `no-new-privileges`;
- ONLY `root` mounted, at `/scratch`, read-only: the mount spec is `type=bind,source=<root>,target=/scratch,readonly`, and test_a_read_mounts_only_its_root_read_only checks there is no other mount.
- A run-tree read is now read-only too; before, its mount was writable.

**The read program:**
- every path step is opened `O_NOFOLLOW` through directory descriptors, so a link anywhere on the path, a non-folder parent, or a final entry that is not a regular file refuses;
- a file with another hard link refuses. This is new for reads and applies to both roots: stricter for the run tree, which R2-3 had left open;
- the size check counts the bytes actually read, not fstat's size, so a session file the running agent grows during the read cannot pass the cap;
- the reply carries `sha256` and `bytes`, and `operator_read` checks both against the decoded data. A mismatch or an off-shape reply is an OwnershipError.

**The 32 MiB cap is a policy value, not a measurement.** A session file or rollout JSONL carries every turn's messages and tool output, and its base64 reply is about 43 MiB on stdout. A larger file refuses loudly and is never truncated. The first walk records the real sizes; re-rule the cap if any comes near it.

**Red first** (`runs/67`; the new tests against `544c6ddb`): test_ownership, 3 errors.

**Mutants** (`mutants/ownership_mutants.py`, `runs/68`): 51 of 51 red, `ALIVE: none`.
- New:
  - a final link followed;
  - a folder link followed;
  - the wrong root mounted (the parent);
  - a write mount;
  - "readonly" spelled away;
  - hard links admitted;
  - the reply hash unchecked;
  - the reply length unchecked;
  - the config cap set to the run tree's;
  - any root kind admitted;
  - a capability added.
- The first run left "read size unchecked" alive, because a second size check (fstat) backed up the bytes-read check. I removed the fstat check. The one bytes-read check is now bound alone, and its mutant is red.

**Runner counts** (Mac, light gate passed; `runs/69-*`): test_ownership Ran 27, OK; test_box_integration Ran 61, OK; copy_manifest and freeze checks ok, 378 files.

### Call contract for drive.py, amended (harness lane)

5. **Mid-take reads of the config folder and the run tree.** After `ownership.grant` and before `ownership.restore`, the host never opens the agent-owned folders. Every read of them goes through:
   `ownership.operator_read(self.runtime, image=spec.image, root_kind="config", root=take.config_dir, rel_path=<relative path>, caller_env=self.caller_env)`
   For the run tree, use `root_kind="run_tree"` and `root=spec.run_tree`.
   - It returns the bytes, or None when the path is absent.
   - It raises OwnershipError on a link, a special file, a hard-linked file, a file over the cap, or a bad reply. The driver records it as an operator step, never as an agent event, and decides the routing; a refused read never yields partial bytes.
   - `rel_path` is normalised and relative; nothing is globbed.
   - To list a sessions folder, a listing program is not part of this slice. Name the files you need, and ask the box lane if a listing is required.
6. **Image:** the take's own image id from its lock, as for grant and restore.

## operator_list for session discovery, and the operator-read review folds O-1 to O-5 (written 12:56 EDT, clock read first)

`d73c43ed` PASSED (`gap4-review-ownership-20261014/review-operator-read.md`). glitch-06 asked that the review's folds go to both operator_read and the new operator_list.

**operator_list**

- Signature: `ownership.operator_list(runtime, *, image, root_kind, run_tree, config_dir, rel_dir, caller_env=None)`.
  - It lists ONE named folder and never recurses; `"."` is the root itself.
  - It returns `[(name, kind)]`, sorted by name bytes, or None when the folder is absent. kind is one of:
    - `"file"`, `"dir"`;
    - `"link"`, reported so that the caller refuses it;
    - `"other"`, for any other type and for any name that is not valid UTF-8.
  - kind comes from an lstat through the folder's descriptor, with every path step opened O_NOFOLLOW. Listing through a link or through a file refuses.
- Names travel base64 and come back byte-exact; a non-UTF-8 name decodes with surrogateescape.
- **`LIST_MAX_ENTRIES` = 4096**, a policy cap. More entries refuse; the list is never truncated.
- The reply carries a sha256 over its canonical JSON (`separators=(",", ":")`), and the host recomputes it. A kind outside the closed set, a name holding "/" or NUL, ".", "..", or a duplicate refuses.
- The container and mount are operator_read's: the agent uid, no capabilities, no network, no new privileges, and only the root, read-only.
- The harness walks the dated folders level by level: `sessions` → `YYYY` → `MM` → `DD`, and `tmp` → `<project>` → `chats`.

**The review folds, applied to both functions**

- **O-2 (credential names).** Under the config root, a path with any part matching preflight's `CREDENTIAL_FILES` (every harness, `auth.json` included) or the `CREDENTIAL_LIKE` backstop refuses before any call. A listing marks such an entry `"other"`.
  - Once the Codex file store lands, `CODEX_HOME/auth.json` holds the login, and the host never receives it.
  - The run tree is not under this rule: its files are the task's, so `p/tokenize.py` reads.
  - The stale `CREDENTIAL_FILES` source text ("so no auth.json is written") now names the 13 Oct subscription amendment (`forced_login_method chatgpt`, `cli_auth_credentials_store file`).
  - **Named for the Codex binding:** preflight's postflight check refuses a config folder holding a credential-like file. With the file store, `auth.json` will always be there, so the binding must rule how postflight treats it before any Codex take.
- **O-1 (torn reads).** The read program compares the file's size, mtime_ns, inode and device before and after the read, and the bytes read against the size. Any change refuses ("the file changed during the read"), with no retry.
  - Tests inject one append, one truncation, and one same-size rewrite mid-read; each refuses.
- **O-3 (the root).** Both functions now take the take's pair (`run_tree`, `config_dir`) and choose the root by kind; the `root=` keyword is gone. The host checks:
  - both are exact absolute paths, existing folders and never links;
  - neither is inside the other;
  - the config folder sits outside every repository.
  - An unknown kind refuses there first.
- **O-5.** The read loop accumulates into a bytearray.
- **O-4 (athena probe).** Gate first: `athena_suite.py list` (no run in progress; every 14 Oct entry has a verdict), 0 athena-suite processes, load 0.02.
  - Under the real `operator_argv(readonly=True)` against the take image (`sha256:336aa8cd…`), as uid 10001, every write form failed `EROFS`: create, append to an existing file, mkdir, unlink and chmod.
  - The scratch folder was 0777 with a 0666 file, so only the mount could stop a write.
  - The read and list programs answered. The control, the same program on a writable mount, wrote.
  - No container was left, and the scratch was removed.
  - Evidence: `stub-take-20261014/operator-readonly-mount-probe-20261014.json` (sha256 `8aeb46f0…`), script `probe_operator_readonly_mount.sh`.

**Red first**

- `runs/70`: the list tests against `d73c43ed`, 13 errors.
- `runs/74`: the list and O-1/2/3 tests against `d73c43ed`, 2 failures and 22 errors.

**Mutants**

- `mutants/ownership_mutants.py`, `runs/72`: 72 of 72 red, `ALIVE: none`, control 0 of 37. Among them:
  - list: a link listed as a dir, recursion (in the program, and admitted by the host), the wrong root mounted, a write mount, listing through a folder link, the program's and the host's entry caps, the reply hash, any kind, duplicates;
  - O-1: a torn read admitted; only the size compared;
  - O-2: a credential path admitted; only the last part checked; the CREDENTIAL_LIKE backstop dropped; a listed credential left readable;
  - O-3: a link root, nesting, the config folder in a repository, any root kind, and the other root mounted.
- Two mutants survived intermediate runs, and each showed a pair of guards backing each other:
  - "read size unchecked": the fstat check was removed, so the bytes-read check stands alone;
  - "list: any root kind": the kind check now lives in `_take_root` alone.
- `runs/73`: the CREDENTIAL_FILES source text reverted, and auth.json dropped from CREDENTIAL_FILES: both red.

### Call contract for drive.py, amended again (harness lane)

5. **Mid-take reads:** `ownership.operator_read(self.runtime, image=spec.image, root_kind="config" | "run_tree", run_tree=spec.run_tree, config_dir=take.config_dir, rel_path=…, caller_env=self.caller_env)`.
   - Read the config folder only after the turn's quiescence. A torn read is an OwnershipError, never retried silently.
   - A credential file's name under the config root refuses.
   - **Run-tree operator reads raise watcher access events** (the read opens files in the watched tree). The ledger's operator steps must name each read, so that the sidecar's events for it are explained and never counted as the agent's.
5b. **Discovery:** `ownership.operator_list(…, root_kind=…, run_tree=…, config_dir=…, rel_dir=…)` lists one folder.
   - Walk the dated folders level by level.
   - Refuse a `"link"` entry; skip `"other"`; read only `"file"` entries through operator_read.
   - None means the folder is absent.

## The walk-token road for Claude1, and Javier's handoff card (written 14:00 EDT, clock read first)

Critical path to the first Claude walk, on Thursday after Claude1 resets at 09:00.
glitch-06 asked for this slice from the CP2 hands card ("paste directly into the separately prepared protected Athena file for that account").

**The location, decided:** `/home/javier/gap4-secrets/claude1/claude-walk-token`, recorded as `run_take.WALK_LOGIN_FILES["claude1"]`.
- It is javier's own home: read_login requires the file to be owned by the reading user, and the driver runs as javier on athena.
- `gap4-secrets` and `claude1` are each mode 700, javier:javier. The file is mode 600 with one link.
- It is outside every repository; `git rev-parse` there fails, and the prepare script refuses inside one.
- It is outside every run tree and config folder. Those belong to the walk's own folders, and exec_argv refuses a login file inside the run tree.
- One folder per account, so a second account (claude2) is a sibling, never a neighbour file in the same folder.

**The file's form:** one line, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN=<token>`, which is read_login's existing format.
- **Departure:** glitch-06 asked for an EMPTY file. The prepare script writes the 24 bytes `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN=` (no value, no newline), so Javier pastes only the token and never types the variable name.
- The card opens nano at line 1, column 25 (GNU nano 8.4 on athena), right after the `=`. Nano ends the line on save.

**The docs (code.claude.com, read 14 Oct 2026):**
- authentication.md (sha256 `c43ef3e8…ce67ac`): "generate a one-year OAuth token with `claude setup-token`"; "It does not save the token anywhere; copy it and set it as the `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` environment variable".
- The same page's precedence item 5: "`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` … A long-lived OAuth token generated by `claude setup-token`. Use this for CI pipelines and scripts".
- cli-reference.md (`0acf910e…`) lists `claude setup-token`, which "Prints the token to the terminal without saving it. Requires a Claude subscription."
- env-vars.md (`8157072e…`) describes the variable.
- `run_take.LOGIN_VARIABLE["claude-code"]` is `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, which the box hands to the client environment alone.

**Two doc-driven guards added:**
- `check_claude_settings` refuses `--bare` ("Bare mode does not read `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`").
- `settings_guard.FORBIDDEN_KEYS` adds `apiKeyHelper`. Its output outranks the OAuth token (precedence item 4 against item 5), so a run-tree settings file carrying it would swap the walk's subscription login for another credential. GARS's shipped settings file does not carry it.

**read_login and the new `check_login_place`:**
- read_login already refused a non-absolute path, a link, a non-regular file, a mode other than 600, another owner, a file inside a repository, a file over 4096 bytes, non-ASCII, anything but one newline-terminated line, a wrong prefix, and a value under 20 characters or with whitespace or quotes. It never carries the value in a message.
- **New: `check_login_place(path, run_tree)`, run by exec_argv before read_login.** It requires that:
  - the file has exactly one link;
  - the file sits outside the run tree;
  - its folder is a folder, never a link, mode 700 and owned by the reading user.
- It is a separate function so that read_login's agreement with `scan_record.read_login` (binding 5) is unchanged. A first attempt inside read_login broke binding 5 and masked read_login's own repository mutant.
- **The tests (`TestWalkLoginPlace`):**
  - a good place hands the fake value to the client environment alone;
  - each of these refuses before any read: a 755 folder, a 750 folder, a second hard link, a folder reached through a link (made 700 with lchmod on macOS, so only the link rule can refuse it), inside the run tree, a relative path, and `--bare`;
  - read_login refuses an empty file, two lines, no newline, the bare token, the prefix alone, mode 640, and a linked file.
  - No refusal carries the value.
- **Named residual:** on Linux, symlink modes are 0777, so there the link rule is also backed by the mode rule.

**The scripts** (`lane-reports/walk-login/`, copied to athena at `/home/javier/gap4-tools/walk-login/`, a 700 folder):
- `prepare_walk_login.py <account>` creates the folders and the file with the modes above.
  - It refuses to run as root, through a link, or inside a git repository.
  - It never overwrites a file that already holds more than the name; it reads only the file's size.
  - It prints paths, modes, owner and size as JSON.
- `verify_walk_login.py <account>` prints only: present, mode ok, owner ok, folder ok, one line, format ok, and a length class (empty, under 20, 20-79, 80-199, 200 or more). It never prints the value or a hash of it, and exits 0 only when all pass.
- `test_walk_login_scripts.py` runs both against a temporary HOME with a planted fake value:
  - prepare, then paste, then verify gives OK, and the box's own `read_login` and `check_login_place` agree;
  - a second prepare leaves a pasted value unchanged;
  - verify refuses a wrong mode, a wrong folder mode, two lines, a short value, a value with a space, the bare token, an empty file and a link;
  - prepare refuses a link folder, a bad account name and a home inside a repository;
  - the fake value never appears in any output. Ran 3, OK.

**Run on athena:**
- Gate: `athena_suite.py list` showed no gars-gap4 run in progress, but 8 athena-suite python processes were running at 17:29Z. I waited until they reached 0 (17:37:55Z), then ran at 17:38:20Z.
- Result: `gap4-secrets` 700, `claude1` 700, `claude-walk-token` regular file 600, javier:javier, one link, 24 bytes, outside any git repository.
- verify before the paste: NOT OK (one line NO, length empty), as designed.
- Evidence: `lane-reports/walk-login/walk-login-prepared-20261014.json (renamed from walk-token-…: the repository's .gitignore line 68, `*token*.json`, guards secret-shaped names; the file holds no token)`, with the scripts' sha256 on athena (`a48d98e4…` prepare, `407500ef…` verify), equal to the committed files.
- No token exists. No session created, read or printed one.

**The card:** `/Users/javrodher/glitch/plans/gap-study-round-4/walk-token-handoff.md` (in the Brain's plans, not in this repository). It has four steps in plain words:
1. `claude setup-token` on the Mac, with the browser signed in to Claude1;
2. `ssh -t athena nano +1,25 /home/javier/gap4-secrets/claude1/claude-walk-token`, paste after the `=`, save, and clear the terminal;

## test_sidecar's process audit: the scanner binding moves to rules_scanner.py (written 14:42 EDT, clock read first)

The merged battery showed four box reds, also red at `d73c43ed`.
test_sidecar (the w4c and w6g lines, and `--process-check`) refused "box/scan_record.py: imports importlib, outside its allowlist".

- **`rules_scanner.real_rules_scanner()`**, new, is the binding. It reads that record, then refuses (`scan_record.NotReady`) in two cases:

- **walk_builders** (the harness lane's file) now takes the record from scan_record and binds it through `rules_scanner.real_rules_scanner()`. A drifted pin still refuses there.

- **The S-1 tests and the record binding are kept**, now against `rules_scanner.real_rules_scanner`.
  - A new test checks that scan_record returns a copy.
  - Another new test, `test_a_drifted_pin_refuses_the_binding`, checks that the binding itself refuses each drift.
- **Mutants** (`mutants/scanner_guard_mutants.py`, `runs/83`): control 0 of 7; 5 of 5 red, `ALIVE: none`.

## The Codex lease postflight (written 15:48 EDT, clock read first)

glitch-06 accepted the W folds, pending a closure check.
Next slice: after a Codex take, postflight must admit exactly the lease's one registered credential and refuse everything else credential-shaped.

**The lease home** is the per-take config folder. It is CODEX_HOME (`TAKE_ENV[codex]`). The owner's 13 Oct subscription amendment (`cli_auth_credentials_store file`) puts the login at `CODEX_HOME/auth.json`.

**`preflight.LEASE_CREDENTIAL = {codex: "auth.json"}`.** For a harness in that table, postflight runs `lease_credential_problems`. Claude Code and Gemini keep the old rule: no credential file at all.

**What postflight admits:** at most one file, `auth.json` at the folder's root, when all of these hold:
- it is a regular file, never a link;
- it has exactly one link;
- its mode is 600;
- it is owned by the host user, as it is after ownership.restore;
- it is at most 1 MiB.

**What it refuses.** Each refusal names the file and the rule, never the contents:
- the registered path as a link or a non-regular file;
- the registered file with a second hard link, another mode or another owner;
- a credential-shaped name anywhere else (preflight's CREDENTIAL_FILES of every harness, or CREDENTIAL_LIKE). That covers a second auth file and a renamed one, such as `auth.json.bak`;
- a file anywhere else whose bytes equal the registered file's: a renamed copy, whatever its name. Only same-size files are read, for comparison only;
- a symbolic link to the registered file, or a link with a credential's name.

**Decision, mine: an absent `auth.json` is admitted** (`admitted_credential: []`). No credential is left, and an aborted take must not fail postflight for it. glitch-06 can rule it should refuse instead.

The record gains `recorded.admitted_credential`. The check's detail reads "only the registered credential auth.json" when it is present.

**Tests** (`test_preflight.TestLeasePostflight`). The planted value is a fake.
- The clean lease home passes, with auth.json admitted. A home with no credential passes too.
- Each of these refuses, naming its file and rule:
  - a second `sessions/auth.json`;
  - `auth.json.bak` (credential-shaped name);
  - `sessions/notes.txt` with the same bytes (innocuous name);
  - a link to auth.json;
  - auth.json itself as a link;
  - mode 644;
  - another owner;
  - a second hard link.
- No record carries the planted contents.
- Claude Code and Gemini still refuse an auth.json.
- In-suite mutants: no lease (red), and a lease for every harness (red).

**Red first** (`runs/91`, the new test against `5c73dab4`): 1 failure.

**Mutants** (`mutants/lease_postflight_mutants.py`, `runs/90`): one per refusal branch. Control 0 of 1; 8 of 8 red, `ALIVE: none`.
- registered path may be a link;
- second hard link admitted;
- any mode admitted;
- any owner admitted;
- links to it admitted;
- second credential-shaped file admitted;
- renamed copy admitted;
- registered file not exempted by path.

**Process audit:** preflight now imports `stat`, using only `S_IMODE`, `S_ISLNK` and `S_ISREG`. It uses only os attributes already in the audit's closed sets. test_run_take's audit is OK, with no allowlist widened.

**Runner counts** (Mac, light gate passed; `runs/92-*`): test_preflight Ran 56, OK; test_run_take Ran 49, OK; test_stub_take Ran 19, OK.

No athena step was needed.

## Review of the lease postflight (2fd8215a + 34b1a3d2), and the folds (14 Oct, 17:22 EDT)

The 17:06 stamp on the O-4 section above should read 17:05; the clock was not re-read for it.

**Review:** a fresh Opus reviewer (opus-medium), read-only, Mac only, fake values only.
Verdict: no MAJOR findings against the spec; four MINOR, three NIT.
It confirmed by running: test_preflight Ran 56, OK; the shipped mutants 9 of 9 red; and probes of each refusal.

**Folded now (in-spec, Mac only):**
- MINOR 1, untested branches: the size limit and the non-regular half of the registered-path rule had no test, and a credential-named link to an innocuous file had none. "One mutant per refusal branch" in `2fd8215a` was untrue. New cases: a 1 MiB + 1 byte `auth.json`, a FIFO and a folder at `auth.json`, and `sessions/token-link` pointing at `config.toml`.
- The hang the reviewer predicted is real: with the non-regular check removed, a FIFO at `auth.json` hung postflight in a blocking open. My first mutant run hung on exactly that mutant and was stopped by pid. A first fix (open with `O_NONBLOCK`, re-check `S_ISREG` with `fstat`) turned test_run_take's process audit red: `os.O_NONBLOCK`, `os.fstat` and `os.close` are not in its closed `OS_ATTRS`. I did not widen the allowlist and reverted that fix. The non-regular refusal before any open is the guard; the mutant runner now stops a hung mutant at 60 s with SIGALRM and counts it red ("hung").
- MINOR 2, a crash instead of a refusal: an unreadable entry (an `lstat` that fails, or a same-size copy candidate the host cannot open) now refuses by path ("unreadable after the take; the lease home must be readable whole") instead of raising. The registered file's own read refuses the same way ("the registered credential is unreadable").
- MINOR 4, a link to a folder above the credential: a symlink whose target is `auth.json` itself or any folder above it (`commonpath([target, auth.json]) == target`) now refuses. That rule replaces the separate "same target" test, which it covers.
- NIT 7, wording: a link at `auth.json` says "never a link"; a FIFO or folder there says "must be a regular file".

**Red first** (`runs/97-redfirst-review-folds-at-6b4b47aa.txt`, the new cases against `6b4b47aa`'s preflight.py): 1 failing test with two problems, the link to the home folder admitted and the unreadable file raising.
The other new cases passed on the old code: they close test gaps, not behaviour gaps.

**Mutants** (`mutants/lease_postflight_mutants.py`, `runs/98`): 13, one per refusal branch now, including the split link and non-regular branches, oversize, links to it or a folder above it, credential-named links, and the unreadable-candidate handler. Control 0 of 1; 13 of 13 red, `ALIVE: none`; "registered path may be non-regular" is red by hanging (stopped at 60 s), as the reviewer predicted.

**Runner counts** (Mac, light gate passed before each; `runs/99-*`, one module at a time after the slip below): test_preflight Ran 56, OK; test_run_take Ran 49, OK (process audit unwidened); test_stub_take Ran 19, OK; test_box_integration Ran 61, OK.

**Not folded; for glitch-06:**
- MINOR 3 widens the spec. Near-copies are admitted: one trailing byte, base64, gzip, the refresh token alone in a file, a copy split in two, and a link to a copy outside the folder. A file or folder named with a token value is admitted, and that value lands verbatim in `recorded.listing`, so in `postflight.json`. The listing channel predates these commits. Whether the take scan catches it depends on production `secrets()` carrying the lease's token values, which neither the reviewer nor I traced. Options: (a) parse `auth.json` and refuse any file whose bytes, or any path component, contains a token value of 16 characters or more; (b) at least have `secrets()` carry the lease token values, with a test that a token-named file fails the scan. This needs glitch-06's ruling.
- NIT 5: the registered file is checked by path and then opened by path, so a process still alive after teardown could swap in a FIFO (the open blocks) or a two-link regular file between the two. Low risk: no process should be alive after teardown. The fix (`O_NONBLOCK`, then `fstat` and a device, inode, link-count and mode check on the descriptor) needs `os.O_NONBLOCK`, `os.fstat` and `os.close` added to test_run_take's `OS_ATTRS`. That widening is glitch-06's call.
- NIT 6: `box/ownership.py`'s O-2 note says the host never reads a credential file's name in the config folder. Postflight now reads `auth.json`'s bytes, for comparison only and never into a record. The docstring should name this one exception; it was left alone so this commit stays inside preflight.

**A slip, mine:** after I stopped the hung mutant run, its loop carried on into the test modules while my second loop started, so for about 10 minutes two runs of the modules overlapped and wrote the same `runs/99-*` files. That broke "one test module at a time". I stopped both test_box_integration runs by pid, confirmed no test process was left, deleted the mixed `runs/99-*` files, and reran mutants and modules once, in sequence.

**Could not check:** athena's case-sensitive Linux filesystem (only APFS was probed), and whether production `HOST_UID` equals the host's `os.getuid()`.

## glitch-06's rulings on the review's open items (14 Oct, 17:42 EDT)

### MINOR 3: the lease's tokens in the exact-value scan

**Trace (a).** Production `secrets()` for a take is `walk_builders.login_secrets` (`evals/gap-study-4/walk_builders.py:159-166`).
It returns one value: the `NAME=<value>` line of a login file, read by `scan_record.read_login`.
The drive and commit scans call it at scan time (`drive.py:2326` for a take, `drive.py:1688` and `:3085` for the commit).
Nothing read a Codex lease's `auth.json`, so the lease's access and refresh tokens were **not** in the scan.
There is also no Codex production road yet: `walk_builders.prepare` (`walk_builders.py:355`) refuses every harness but Claude Code, and `run_take` refuses Codex with CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_READY (`box/run_take.py:128`, `:753`).

**The change.**
- `scan_record.read_lease_tokens(path)` reads the registered `auth.json` and returns every `access_token` and `refresh_token` value found anywhere in its JSON. It refuses (LoginError, naming the rule, never the contents) unless:
  - the path is absolute and the file is a plain file (never a link or special file), mode 600, owned by its reader, at most 1 MiB;
  - it is JSON, and each key is found, with single-line strings of at least 20 characters.
- `walk_builders.lease_secrets(auth_path)` is a Codex take's `secrets()`. It reads the file at take start; with no lease file, the take never starts. It reads again at each scan and adds any refreshed values.
- **A departure, named:** unlike `login_secrets`' "never held", the values read are kept for that take's scans. A refresh rewrites `auth.json`, and the first values may already be in the records.
- **No inode binding here, named:** a first version also bound the read to the check by `fstat` device and inode. test_sidecar's closed vocabulary for `scan_record.py` turned red on `fstat`, `st_dev` and `st_ino` (`runs/103-test_sidecar.txt`, 2 failures). Your NIT 5 widening covered only test_run_take's `OS_ATTRS` for preflight, so I removed the binding rather than widen a second allowlist. The reader now opens the file as `read_login` does: `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, after the `lstat` checks, with no inode binding. Widening test_sidecar's vocabulary to restore it is your call.
- **Not verified:** the key names follow the Codex CLI's `auth.json` as described in the 13 Oct amendment and the binary's strings (`access_token`, `refresh_token`). I read no real `auth.json`. If either key is missing on the first real lease, the reader refuses, so the scan never runs short of them.
- **Wiring is deferred to the lease road:** the Codex driver does not exist yet, so `lease_secrets` is built and tested but called by no road. The lease road must pass it as `secrets=`.

**The listing channel (b).** `postflight.json` already sits in the take folder's closed map with scan kind `preflight` (`drive.py:1820`). That kind is not maskable, so an exact-value hit there refuses the commit, as in every other non-agent record.
New test: a lease home with a folder named with the synthetic access token. Postflight admits it, because the name rule cannot see it, and the listing carries it. The record goes through `scan_record.scan` with `lease_secrets` as the secrets, and the commit is refused for `postflight.json` (kind token). The value is in no result field, and the file is left as written, never masked.

**Near-copies (c), a named residual, no heuristic.** The exact-value search does not find:
- a base64 or gzip copy of a token;
- a token split across files or lines;
- a token with a trailing byte added, or inside a renamed copy that is not byte-identical;
- the refresh token alone, re-encoded.

As with Claude's W-3 residual, what bounds it is this:
- the box allows egress only to the vendor;
- the exact-value mask is armed on every record;
- the config folder is deleted after the commit.

**Red first** (`runs/100-redfirst-lease-tokens-at-dac954f3.txt`): 5 errors, every new test (no reader, no `lease_secrets`).

**Mutants** (`mutants/lease_tokens_mutants.py`, final run `runs/106`): 10 of 10 red, none alive. They cover:
- only the access token searched;
- a missing key admitted;
- a short value admitted;
- a link or special file admitted;
- any mode admitted;
- any owner admitted;
- an oversize file admitted;
- no read at take start;
- the take-start values dropped at the scan;
- `postflight.json` given a maskable kind.

### NIT 5: the registered file read is the file checked

- `OS_ATTRS` gains exactly `O_NONBLOCK`, `fstat` and `close`, each named in a comment in test_run_take saying why. Nothing else was added.
- `_read_capped(path, cap, checked)` opens with `O_NONBLOCK`, then `fstat`s the descriptor. It requires the same device, inode, link count, mode (which carries the file type) and owner as the caller's `lstat`. It closes the descriptor and raises OSError otherwise. Both callers, the registered file and each same-size copy candidate, pass their `lstat`.
- The registered file's refusal now reads "the registered credential is unreadable, or changed between its check and its read".
- New test (`TestLeasePostflight.check_read`): `auth.json` is swapped right after the check's `lstat` (the second `lstat` of it; the first is `lexists`). Two swaps are tried: a same-bytes regular file with a second link, and a FIFO. Each must be refused by file and rule, without hanging.
- **Red first** (`runs/102-redfirst-swap-at-dac954f3.txt`): against `dac954f3`'s preflight.py, the run hung in the FIFO swap and was stopped by SIGINT at 120 s (exit 124).

### NIT 6: the O-2 docstring

`box/ownership.py`'s `credential_name` docstring now names postflight's compare-only read of `auth.json` as the one exception to "the host never reads or lists a credential file in the config folder".
It also says why the commit scan's own read (`lease_secrets`) is not an operator read: it hands the values to the exact-value search only, as `read_login` does.

### My gap: the registered file's unreadable branch

New case in `check_read`: `_read_capped` raising PermissionError for `auth.json` must refuse, naming `auth.json` and "unreadable", and never raise.

**Lease postflight mutants** (`runs/104`): 15 of 15 red, none alive. Two are new: "the read not bound to the check" and "registered unreadable raises".

## The review of b9f876b5 and a3124c1f: PASS, and the folds (14 Oct, 18:01 EDT)

The independent review came back PASS: no MAJOR, and no MINOR that loosens a check. glitch-06 ruled the folds below.

**MINOR 1, the device and inode are pinned.** A third swap case replaces `auth.json` right after the check with a fresh regular file. It has the same bytes, one link, the same mode and the same owner, so only the inode differs. The file is made beside `auth.json` and renamed over it, and the test asserts that only the inode differs.
A new mutant compares only `(st_nlink, st_mode, st_uid)` in `_read_capped`.
- **Red first** (`runs/108`, the mutants against 4c145f74's tests): that mutant is ALIVE, which is the gap.
- With the new case it is red (`runs/110`).

**MINOR 2, the reader never crashes.** `read_lease_tokens` now:
- catches RecursionError, alongside UnicodeDecodeError and ValueError, around `json.loads` and the key walk, and refuses with "not JSON within the nesting the reader walks". It is caught through its base class RuntimeError: test_sidecar's closed set of free names for `scan_record.py` turned red on the name `RecursionError` (`runs/111-test_sidecar-first-recursionerror-name.txt`), but already holds `RuntimeError`. So no closed set is widened;
- maps an OSError from `os.read` to LoginError("the lease file cannot be read").

Tests:
- a lease file of 100,000 nested arrays;
- the key walk raising RecursionError;
- a folder swapped in after the `lstat`: the open succeeds on a folder, then the read fails.

Each refuses by rule. **Red first** (`runs/109`, against 4c145f74): RecursionError raised out of `json.loads`.
Two new mutants, "deep nesting crashes" and "an unreadable open file crashes", are red (`runs/110`).

**MINOR 3, the refresh residual, named.** `lease_secrets` re-reads `auth.json` at each scan, so a refreshed token is added only when it is still present at scan time. A value refreshed and then replaced again before a scan is never read, so it is not in the exact-value search. The docstring now says this.

**Lease-road design rule (glitch-06, ruled 14 Oct):** the token-refresh endpoint is never in a take's egress. Any refresh happens on the host between turns, and the host captures the refreshed values into the take's secret set at refresh time. Under that rule the residual above cannot arise inside a take.

**NIT 6:** the listing test reads `postflight.json` with `Path(...).read_bytes()`.

## glitch-06's rulings, and the Codex lease road in run_take (14 Oct, 18:11 EDT)

**Catching RuntimeError for RecursionError: accepted** (glitch-06). It fails closed into a refusal, and test_sidecar's closed name set is not widened.

**The split** (glitch-06):
- `box/run_take.py` is this lane's: the Codex exec branch behind the closed gate, and lease placement.
- `walk_builders.py`, `drive.py`, and the NIT 4 and NIT 5 tests are the harness lane's.

**OS_ATTRS is not widened.** Placement uses only names already in it.

### What this lane built: the gate and the placement

- **`CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_READY = False`** (`box/run_take.py:131`): the lease road's gate, committed CLOSED. It opens only after Javier's Codex2 login and the first real walk.
- **`codex_lease_gate()`** (`:913`): raises `NotReady(CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_READY)` while the gate is closed.
- **`place_lease(lease_src, config_dir) -> str`** (`:919`), behind the gate. It copies the registered lease file into the per-take config folder as `auth.json` (`LEASE_FILE`) and returns that path.
  - **The source must be:** an absolute regular file (never a link) with one link, mode 600, owned by the reading user, outside every repository, and 1 byte to 1 MiB (`LEASE_MAX_BYTES`).
  - **The folder must be:** an absolute folder (never a link), mode 700, owned by the reading user, outside every repository, and not already holding `auth.json`.
  - **The copy:** `os.open(dst, O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW, 0o600)`, then `fdopen`, `flush` and `fsync`.
  - **The read-back `lstat` must show:** a regular file, one link, mode 600, owned by the reading user, and the source's size.
  - **A mode narrowed by the umask** (for example 400 under umask 277) refuses. There is no chmod, so it fails closed.
  - **Every refusal** is LoginRefused, naming the file and the rule, never the contents. A refused copy stays in the config folder, which is never launched and is removed with the take's other config files.
- **Red first** (`runs/113`, against `63c3ff80`): no gate attribute and no `place_lease`, so 1 error and 1 failure.
- **Tests** (`TestLeasePlacement`, two tests):
  - The gate is closed in the committed module, and a closed gate places nothing.
  - With the gate patched open, a lease is placed, and each refusal names its file and rule. That covers the source (relative, missing, a link, mode 644, two links, oversize, empty, in a repository, another owner), the folder (relative, missing, a link, mode 755, in a repository, already holding `auth.json`), and the read-back (narrowed by the umask, another owner, a second link, short of the source's size).
- **Mutants** (`mutants/lease_placement_mutants.py`, `runs/114`): 20 of 20 red, none alive, one per refusal branch.
  - The first run left "any source owner admitted" alive: the folder's owner check refused with the same word. The test now names the source's own rule, and the mutant is red.
  - Three OSError branches have no mutant: the source read, the write, and the read-back `lstat`. No test reaches them without a race, and each already refuses by rule.

### The Codex exec branch: not built, and why (needs glitch-06's ruling)

`exec_argv` still refuses Codex (`NotReady`, `:752`). A lease-based exec cannot be added inside `run_take` alone without three spec calls:
1. **The take environment row.** `TAKE_ENV[CODEX]` still carries `CODEX_API_KEY` as its credential, which is the API-key route the owner's 13 Oct amendment forbids. `LOGIN_VARIABLE` is derived from that row, and preflight, `scan_record.read_login`'s allowed names and `walk_builders.login_name` read it. A lease exec has no environment credential, so the row must drop it and `LOGIN_VARIABLE` must treat Codex apart. That touches other modules and lanes.
2. **The in-container launcher.** `LAUNCHER_SOURCE` always copies one credential from the exec's environment (`e[k]=os.environ[k]`). A credential-less Codex turn needs a launcher variant, for example a reserved `-` name that copies nothing. That changes the program that runs inside the image, which the stub-take review covered.
3. **The billing-route variables.** `VENDOR_BILLING_ROUTE_VARIABLES[CODEX]` is PENDING, so `take_environment` refuses Codex anyway (NotReady). It needs naming from the Codex documentation at the pinned version.

I stopped there rather than improvise any of the three.

### For the harness lane: the run_take contract to call

- Before launch, after `make_config_dir(parent)`:
  - `dst = run_take.place_lease(lease_src, cfg)`, which returns `cfg/auth.json`;
  - `secrets = walk_builders.lease_secrets(dst)`: the take-start read. With no lease file, there is no take.
- Pass that same `secrets` as `secrets=` to both `drive.walk_driver` and `drive.WalkRepo` for a Codex row. This is the NIT 4 test.
- Postflight then admits exactly `cfg/auth.json` (`preflight.LEASE_CREDENTIAL`). The listing case through drive's commit scan is the NIT 5 test.
- While `CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_READY` is False, `place_lease` raises `NotReady(CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_NOT_READY)`, so a Codex row stops before anything is written. Tests open the gate with `mock.patch.object(run_take, "CODEX_SUBSCRIPTION_READY", True)`.
- **Glitch-06's refresh rule:** the token-refresh endpoint is never in a take's egress. Any refresh happens on the host between turns, and the host adds the refreshed values to the take's secret set at refresh time. The road must call `secrets()` after each host-side refresh.
